artersv ·
July 1, 2013
Honorable Mayor and City Council:
A balanced budget for the City of Cartersville for the 2013-14 fiscal year is submitted as
required by the Cartersville Municipal Code. This budget focuses on controlling expenditures as
the city deals with a recovering economy, without reducing services to our citizens. The budget
features a use of general fund reserves, no salary adjustments, a decrease in the number of
staff positions, an increase in the cost of commodities, limited replacement of rolling stock and
other equipment, and funding for renewal and extensions of the city's utility infrastructure.
Department heads are to be commended for their hard work In helping to balance the budget
and their staffs comm itment to continue providing dependable service, while preserving an
exceptional quality of life.
The city's total budget for 2014 is $145,243,235, a decrease of 0.06% from last year's budget.
The city's 2014 budget has decreased due to the declining revenue projections in several funds.
The decrease in revenue projections were offset by decreases in the Cartersville School System
property tax appropriation and capital expenses along with increases in personnel expenses,
operating expenses, and commodity expenses, debt service expenses, and transfers to the
general fund. The largest of these decreases is $10,922,720 in the capital expenses and is due
to decreased spending in SPLOST funds. The larger increases in expenses include $6,354,350
related to cost of commodities and $4,342,845 related to debt service expenses. The FY14
budget will decrease by a total of $86,185 from the prior year to $145.2 million,

FY14 BUDGET-STAFFING & SALARY ADJUSTMENT

Personnel costs and employee benefits represent 16.6% of the city's FY 2014 budget. The FY
2014 budget includes no salary adjustments. No new staff positions were added to the FY14
budget, while two positions were deleted. The anticipated cost of our employee health care
plan and dental care plan are expected to increase. In order to defray some of the cost of the
increases to the city, the city elected to increase the employee cost of these insurance plans.
Also to help defray the rising cost of employee benefits, the pension plan contributions made by
the city on the employee's behalf will decrease to 14.0%. The net result of the changes
mentioned above resulted in an increase in overall salary and benefit cost of $362,555 from last
year's budget.

TOTAL COST OF SALARY & BENEFIT ADJUSTMENTS - - FY14
(Tabled)

As stated above, the FY 2014 budget includes no additional staff, and a decrease of two staff
positions. This will bring the total budgeted positions for FY 2014 to three-hundred fifty-eight
(358) full-time permanent positions.
In FY 2009, the city's level of employment peaked at 367 full time positions. At the end of FY
2009, the employment level decreased by 19, accomplished by attrition and retirements. As a
result, the employment level for FY 2010 decreased to 348 full time positions and remained at
the same level through FY 2012. In FY 2013, the level of employment increased to 360 with
the hiring of twelve firefighters. With the deletion of the two positions, the FY 2014
employment level will decrease to a level of 358 full time positions.

II.

FY14 BUDGET - SPLOST & CAPITAL PROJECTS

FY 2014 SPLOST Projects
The SPLOST (Special Purpose Local Option Sales Tax) 2003 projects will be funded within the
FY 2014 budget through interest earnings and a $790,000 carry-over of SPLOST funds from
previous years' collection of the SPLOST 2003 funds. The SPLOST 2007 projects will be funded
in the FY 2014 budget with the one-cent (SPLOST) collections and interest earnings.

As shown above, the overall city contributions to the Community Agency Funding for FY 2014
totals $699,200. In addition to the above, the Cartersville - Bartow County Tourism Council is
funded at $228,145 through Hotel/Motel tax collections. In FY 2014, the Cartersville Building
Authority will be funded $176,555 for debt service payments on the industrial park. Beginning
in FY 2014, the anticipated amount required from the General Fund for the debt of the
industrial park will be reduced to zero because of a SPLOST referendum voted in by the citizens
of Cartersville.

IV.

FY14 BUDGET - FEE & RA TE INCREASES

There is no property tax increases included in the FY2013-14 budget. However, the Water and
Sewer Fund revenue projections include three user increases. They are: a 5.0% increase in
residential customer rates; a 15% increase in the residential base rate; and a 50% increase in
the fire service fee rates for in-city and out-of-city commercial customers. While the water and
sewer department strives to avoid yearly rate increases, growing operation and maintenance
expenses necessitate the collection of additional revenue. Even with the rate increase, the city
water and sewer rate will remain one of the lowest residential rates in the surrounding counties.
Several years ago, capacity fees decreased to one half of the rate. With the approval of the FY

6

2014 budget, the capacity fees will revert back to 100% beginning January 1, 2014. Capacity
fees are used strictly to fund expansion of infrastructure associated with growth, while the
approved water and sewer rate increase will be used solely to fund the operation and
maintenance costs of the existing infrastructure. Aside from the water and sewer rate increase,
there are no additional rate or fee increases included in the FY2013-14 budget.

V.

FY 14 BUDGET- SUMMARY

The budget for FY 2014 of $145.2 million for the City of Cartersville shows a decrease of
$86,185 or 0.06% less than the previous fiscal year budget. The decrease in the budget is
driven by a decrease in capital expenses and Cartersville School system tax appropriations.
Along with these decreases, personnel expenses, operating expenses, purchase of commodities
expenses, debt service expenses, and transfers to the General Fund all increased. The following
table shows the difference between FY14 and FY13 for each fund. The decrease in the General
Fund is due to a decrease in revenues, and is offset by decreases in school board tax
appropriations and capital expenses, while personnel expenses and operating expenses
increased. The Electric Fund increase is due to a projected increase in overall revenues offset
mainly by increases in personnel expenses, operating expenses, and the purchase of
commodities. The Gas Fund increase is mainly due to an increase in projected natural gas costs
over the prior fiscal year. Solid Waste Fund's personnel expenses have increased while the
operating expenses and capital expenses decreased from the prior year. FY 2014 will see the
recycling program continue and will eventually see a decrease in the solid waste being buried at
the landfill. From an environmental standpoint, this is the right action for solid waste and
should, in time, as the program continues to grow, also substantially decrease the city's landfill
costs. The Stormwater Fund changes are related to increases in personnel expenses and
capital project expenses. The decrease in the Water & Sewer Fund is due to decreases in debt
service expenses and capital expenses, while personnel expenses and operating expenses both
increased. SPLOST capital projects are approved by voters and budgeted as funding is
available. Several 2003 capital projects have been completed in prior years and current year
expense projections are less than FY 2013, while 2007 capital projects are funded with their
sixth year of SPLOST collections, and expense projections have decreased over the prior fiscal
year due to the completion of several large projects in FY 2013.

The FY 2014 budget features use of General Fund reserves in the amount of $319,180 and no
salary increases across all funds. The FY 2014 budget contains a decrease in overall
employment positions ending with a total position count of three hundred and fifty-eight (358)
full time positions.
The budget includes continued funding for service contracts between the city and various
community agencies that supply services to the community. These include funding of the
Cartersville-Bartow County Library, the Cartersville - Bartow Convention and Visitors Bureau,
and the Chamber of Commerce. In addition, continued funding of the Bartow-Cartersville Joint
Development Authority in the amount of $155,500 is also included.
The FY 2014 budget includes several rate increases for water and sewer customers, but no
increase in the city property tax millage rate. Although there is a small increase in the water
and sewer rates for residential customers, it, along with the cities other utility rates, will remain
at or below any of the surrounding counties or private utility service providers.

In summary, this budget is a conseivative financial plan, which includes funds to maintain and
operate the city so that we can continue to provide citizens with police and fire protection,
culture and recreation, and other general government seivices. It is a plan and tool, which
provides guidance and gives us the ability to meet our goal of "providing dependable seivice
while preseiving an exceptional quality of life." As in past years, the FY 2014 budget has
incorporated low cost, dependable utility seivices for the citizens of the City of Cartersville.
The budget's production and the operation of the city would not be possible without the effort
and diligence of the city's various departments and those competent and capable employees
who staff them.

>---.•ty of Cartersvill
July 1, 2009
Honorable Mayor and City Council:
A balanced budget for the City of Cartersville for the 2009-2010 fiscal year is submitted as
required by the Cartersville Municipal Code. This budget focuses on controlling expenditures as
we deal with the continued downturn in the economy, without reducing services to our citizens.
The budget features no salary adjustments for any permanent full-time employees, a large
reduction in the cost of commodities, limited replacement of rolling stock and other equipment,
and funding for renewal and extensions of the city's utility infrastructure.
Department heads are to be commended for their hard work in helping to balance the budget
and their staffs commitment to continue providing dependable service while preserving an
exceptional quality of life to meet the needs of the citizens of Cartersville.
The city's total budget for 2010 is $142,266,325, a decrease of 16.06% over last year's budget.
Typically, a budget increases from year to year. However, for the first time in many years, the
city's FY 2010 budget has decreased due to reductions in the revenue projections caused by the
continued economic slowdown. The reductions in revenue projections were offset by decreases
in personnel expenses, operating costs, commodities and Cartersville School System property
tax appropriation. The largest of these decreases is in natural gas commodities at $22.6 million
over the prior year, which is offset slightly by a projected increase in electricity commodity
expense of $3.1 million, leaving a net commodities expense decrease of $19.5 million. The FYlO
budget will decrease by a total of $27,222,795 from the prior year to $142.3 million.

being filled and several employee retirements. Operating expenditures are projected to be
$1,460,235 less in FYlO due to decreased gasoline and diesel fuel expenses, and decreased
utility and facility maintenance costs throughout city departments. Projected commodity
expenses as detailed above are estimated to decrease by $19,553,475 from FY09. Debt service
expenses, primarily attributable to water and sewer bonds, are projected to decrease by
$1,015. Capital projects expenses are projected to decrease by $4,280,390 and the school
board appropriation will decrease by $1,223,010. A projected decrease in transfers from utili1:J1

I.

FY10 BUDGET- STAFFING & SALARY ADJUSTMENT

Personnel costs and employee benefits represent 15.3% of the city's FYlO budget. The FY10
budget includes no salary adjustments for any permanent full-time employees. Instead, several
vacant positions were not filled and 15 employees took early retirement options in the FY 2010
budget. On advice from our actuary in an attempt to fully fund the pension plan, there is an
increase in the pension plan contributions made by the city on the employee's behalf of 1.15%
from 10.35% to 11.5%. The net result of the changes mentioned above resulted in a decrease
in overall salary and benefit cost of $561,855 from last year's budget.

TOTAL COST OF SALARY ADJUSTMENT - - FY10

(Tabled)
·

Cost of Salary Decreases

s: fa--

•

As stated above, the FY10 budget includes a reduction in the number of positions. The
positions involved are vacant positions (4) along with fifteen (15) employees that have chosen
to retire early. These positions will be looked at to decide if they need to be filled, and, if they
are deemed to be a necessary position, the necessary adjustments will be made. This will bring
the total budgeted positions for FY10 to three-hundred forty-eight (348) full-time pennanent
positions.
Over the last thirteen years, the city has experienced a significant growth in the budget of 79%
or $62.8 million due to increased operational expenses, citywide population growth and
increased service area of our utilities. This has pushed our employee staffing level over the
three-hundred and twenty-one (321) positions that were funded in the FY97 budget before t he
cutbacks of 1997. Since FY97 the city has been operating in an efficient manner financially, but
due to the increased demands on city services, staffing levels have had to increase to meet
these needs.

FY10 BUDGET - SPLOST & CAPITAL PROJECTS

The SPLOST 2003 projects will be funded within the FYlO budget through interest earnings and
a $3,630,000 carry over of SPLOST funds from previous years' collection of the SPLOST - 2003
funds. The SPLOST 2007 projects will be funded in the FYlO budget with the one-cent Special
Purpose Local Option Sales Tax (SPLOST) collections, interest earnings, and $2, 150,000 issue of
debt for the construction of the joint police/ fire facility.

As shown above, the overall city contributions to the Community Agency Funding for FY10
decreased by $83,000 to $790,200 due to the Indian Festival not being held in FY 2010 and a
reduction in funding for the Library, the Joint Development Authority, the Wilkins Youth
Association, the Gatekey Scholarship Program, and the Bartow Health Access. In addition to the
above, the Cartersville - Bartow County Tourism Council is funded at $81,200 through
Hotel/Motel tax collections.

IV.

FY10 BUDGET- FEE & RATE INCREASES

The Water and Sewer Fund revenue projections include an increase of 5.3% in customer rates.
In addition, the Gas Fund revenue projections also include an increase of 5.0% or less for its
customers. There are no other additional fee increases or property tax increases for city
purposes included in this budget.

6

V.

FY 10 BUDGET- SUMMARY

The budget for FYlO of $142.3 million for the City of Cartersville shows a decrease of
$27,222,795 or 16.06% less than the previous budget. The decrease in the budget is driven by
a decrease in natural gas commodities, operating expenses, capital expenses, and Cartersville
School System property tax appropriation. The following table shows the difference between
FYlO and FY09 for each fund. The major difference in the general fund is a reduction in
revenues, a reduction in personnel expenses and operating expenses, and a decrease in school
system property tax appropriation. The Electric Fund increase is due to a projected increase in
the large and extra large power industrial customers continued electric consumption over the
prior year. The Gas Fund difference is due to a decrease in projected natural gas costs over the
prior fiscal year. Solid Waste Fund capital expenses have increased from the prior year and
Stormwater Fund changes are related to a decrease in capital project expenses. The decrease
in Water & Sewer Fund is due to a reduction in debt service costs and capital projects. SPLOST
capital projects are approved by voters and budgeted as funding is available. Several 2003
capital projects have been completed in prior years and current year expense projections are
less than FY09, while 2007 capital projects are in their second year of funding, and, therefore
expense projections have increased over the prior fiscal year.

The FY10 budget features no salary increase for any permanent full-time employees. However,
it does include a 1.15% increase in funding for the pension plan. The FYlO budget features a
reduction of four (4) vacant positions and fifteen (15) employee retirements bringing the total
positions to three hundred and forty-eight (348).
The budget includes continued funding for service contracts between the city and various
community agencies that supply services to the community. These include funding of the

7

Cartersville-Bartow County Library, Downtown Development Authority, the Cartersville - Bartow
Convention and Visitors Bureau, and the Chamber of Commerce. In addition, continued funding
of the Bartow-Cartersville Joint Development Authority in the amount of $157,500 is also
included.
The FYlO budget includes a rate increase for water and sewer customers as well as a rate
increase for gas customers, but no increase in the city property tax mfllage rate.

Again, in summary, this budget is a fiscally conservative financial plan, which includes funds to
maintain and operate the city so that we can continue to provide citizens with police and fire
protection, culture and recreation, and other general government services. It is a plan and tool,
which provides guidance and gives us the ability to meet our goal of "providing dependable
service while preserving an exceptional quality of life."
The budget's production and the operation of the city would not be possible without the effort
and diligence of the city's various departments and those competent and capable employees
who staff them.
